NUM	<p>09e3_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene A</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #7f5400;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006789;">Gene H</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006789;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006789;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7f3e95;">Gene K</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #7f3e95;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f3e95;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>0cd5_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #016f4d;">Gene A</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #016f4d;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #016f4d;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #3951c3;">Gene D</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #3951c3;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3951c3;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9e304d;">Gene T</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #9e304d;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>c52b_bbfa</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene H</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #6d5d08;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006d65;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene N</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #8f3974;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>684e_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #576517;">Gene A</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #576517;"><i>artsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #576517;">Gene A</span> has wings that are colorful and distinctive patterns.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #0e6a76;">Gene J</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #0e6a76;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #0e6a76;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene R</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #8f3974;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>9896_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene M</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #7f5400;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006d65;">Gene R</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #006d65;"><i>rusty</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006d65;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #7f3e95;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #7f3e95;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene M</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #8a4b19;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene B</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #6d5d08;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006789;">Gene D</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #006789;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006789;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene N</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #8f3974;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>d96f_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene M</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>mushy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene M</span> feels soft to the touch and unusually squishy, unlike the usual firmness.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #576517;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #576517;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #576517;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene Y</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #6648ab;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>3536_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene K</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #0e6a76;">Gene J</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #0e6a76;"><i>jerky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #0e6a76;">Gene J</span> moves in rapid and sudden movements, displaying an unpredictable flight pattern.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene X</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #6648ab;"><i>xanthic</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene X</span> has a fluorescent bright yellow coloring.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; border: 2px solid black;"><tr><th align="center" style="border: 1px solid black; NUM	<p>76f3_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene C</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #8a4b19;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene C</span> shows behavior where it always maintains a close distance to other flies.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006d65;">Gene D</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #006d65;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006d65;">Gene D</span> appears moist, with its body covered in tiny droplets of water.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene N</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #6648ab;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #6d5d08;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene F</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #7f5400;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006789;">Gene R</span> has a reddish-brown color, much like rusted iron metal.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #8f3974;">Gene W</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #8f3974;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>a08d_e890</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene B</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>bumpy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene B</span> has a skin texture that is not smooth, but rough with small bumps all over.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #576517;">Gene H</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #576517;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #576517;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006789;">Gene K</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #006789;"><i>kidney</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006789;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>f81c_8eb4</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #7f5400;">Gene D</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #7f5400;"><i>dewy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #0e6a76;">Gene K</span> has a body shape that is curved, similar to a kidney bean.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #6648ab;">Gene Y</span></strong> is analogous to the '<span style="color: #6648ab;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. NUM	<p>a38e_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene H</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #6d5d08;"><i>horsey</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene H</span> is quite big and strong-looking, much larger than your typical fruit fly.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #23619c;">Gene N</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #23619c;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #23619c;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #9e304d;">Gene Y</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #9e304d;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #9e304d;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #6d5d08;">Gene F</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #6d5d08;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene E</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #8a4b19;"><i>eery</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene E</span> appears to have something off, crooked limbs and other twisted appendages.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #366c2f;">Gene T</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #366c2f;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #366c2f;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006789;">Gene Y</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #006789;"><i>yucky</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006789;">Gene Y</span> gives off an unpleasant odor and has a generally unappealing look.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; NUM	<p>9dad_fc1a</p> <h4>Three-Point Test Cross Problem</h4><p>A test cross is a way to explore the relationship between genes and their respective alleles. It is a useful tool for genetic mapping and deciphering the inheritance of traits. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene N</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>nerdy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene N</span> has large, prominent eyes that stand out, much like thick-rimmed glasses.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #576517;">Gene T</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #576517;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #576517;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #23619c;">Gene W</span></strong> is associated with the '<span style="color: #23619c;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #23619c;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #a0351f;">Gene C</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #a0351f;"><i>chummy</i></span>' phenotype. Specifically, a three-point test cross examines three (3) genes at the same time to learn about their assortment in gamete formation.</p><p>A standard three-point test cross involves crossing a heterozygous organism for all three genes with an organism that is homozygous recessive for all three genes</p> <p>For this problem, a test cross using a fruit fly (<i>Drosophila melanogaster</i>) heterozygous for three genes was conducted to understand their genetic interactions.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene P</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #8a4b19;"><i>prickly</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #8a4b19;">Gene P</span> is covered with sharp bristles, giving it a spiky texture.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #366c2f;">Gene T</span></strong> is linked with the '<span style="color: #366c2f;"><i>tipsy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #366c2f;">Gene T</span> moves in an erratic path, suggesting a lack of coordination, as if intoxicated.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #3951c3;">Gene W</span></strong> is related to the '<span style="color: #3951c3;"><i>waxy</i></span>' phenotype. A fruit fly that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #3951c3;">Gene W</span> has a thick protective layer that is water resistant and opague.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; Finding a decimal in your answer, such as 5.5, indicates a mistake was made. Please provide your answer as a complete number without fractions or decimals.</li><li><i>Important Tip 2:</i> Your answer should be written as a numerical value only, with no spaces, commas, or units such as "cM" or "map units". For example, if the distance is fifty one centimorgans, simply write "51".</li></ul></p>	60.00000000	0.10000000
